# How to Think Clearly In The Era Of AI: Full Course (5 Hours)

## Creator description

🔥 Join Maker School & get customer #1 guaranteed: https://skool.com/makerschool/about
💎 Get the Thinking Clearly doc 100% free: https://skool.com/maker-zero/about
➡️ If this is your first video: I'm Nick Saraev. I run LeftClick, an AI growth agency. We've done work for large brands you're likely familiar with, like Mr. Beast, Anthropic, OpenAI, and a few others.

In plain English: I help people build systems that generate leads, close deals, and scale businesses, mostly using new AI models like Claude. I also make videos about how to do all of that here on YouTube.

It hasn't always been this way. My family immigrated from a Soviet bloc country (People's Republic of Bulgaria) in the 90s. They worked extremely hard, and we were broke for a very long time. I grew up wishing for more security, stability, and freedom over my own life.

I don't have a programming degree, and never took formal education for IT or workflows. I learned it all myself from YouTube videos like you're watching now.

As for my path: I started my first business at 19 in college. We threw events (mostly parties) at campus bars, and charged people for entry. 

That failed to provide me enough money to pay my bills, so at 21, I started another business, a door-to-door marketing agency.

From there, every year, I would start several companies, over 10 in total. I did this in a variety of industries, like e-commerce, photography, videography, SaaS, credit repair, etc. Each grew slightly more profitable, until, at 25, I would start 1SecondCopy, a content writing company. A friend and I eventually scaled that to over $90,000/m. It was my first taste of financial independence, and the reason I'm here today.

Since starting my journey, I've grown a fair bit of capital. To diversify my income, at 30, I began investing in other businesses and advising companies on how to grow in the AI era. These include SaaS businesses, like Clairvo, agencies like Dental Connect, and more. 

Collectively, the businesses that I own outright or are a part of generate over $10 million per year. This returns far more than the 5-10% you would get in the stock market, but also requires a lot of my active time. There's a tradeoff there, but I greatly prefer active investing, where I have control over the entities I'm a part of.

It wasn't easy to get here. But I did in the end! For anyone on their own entrepreneurial journey: I wish you luck, happiness, and perseverance. You can make it!

Summary ⤵️
This is the Clear Thinking Course, a magnum opus I put together over the course of the last few days where I dumped everything that I know about thinking clearly and making effective decisions into one place

This course represents around a decade of my life of learning various thinking techniques on both the hardware side, e.g. biological optimizations, and software side, e.g. thinking techniques and rationality principles

You will learn about things like expected value methodology, Bayes' theorem, the planning fallacy, local and global maxima and minima, the power law, the chain law, exploration versus exploitation, and more!

I hope you enjoy.

[01:55:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=6952s) probability of tails is 50%. So, there's

[01:55:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=6957s) following along with me. In expected

[03:52:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13968s) [gasps]

[03:52:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13969s) One approach to do this is to use what's

[03:52:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13971s) called a planning fallacy multiplier.

[03:52:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13974s) This is where you take your inside

[03:52:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13976s) estimate, which is the one that you

[03:52:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13978s) would have come up with using the

[03:53:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13980s) simulation in your head of all the ways

[03:53:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13982s) that things are going to go right, and

[03:53:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13983s) then just multiply that amount of time

[03:53:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13985s) that you came up with by some constant

[03:53:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13987s) number. Believe it or not, that has to

[03:53:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13990s) be one of the simplest and easiest ways

[03:53:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13991s) to readjust for your internal bias the

[03:53:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13994s) wrong way. In practice, many people

[03:53:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13996s) multiply their planning fallacy

[03:53:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=13998s) estimates by three. Others do it by two.

[03:53:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14001s) And in my case, I do it by about 1.5.

[03:53:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14004s) And I do it by about 1.5 because I

[03:53:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14006s) typically run small projects with very

[03:53:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14008s) few people involved. So I know that I am

[03:53:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14010s) usually the majority of the variability

[03:53:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14012s) in the outcome. Now, it's important to

[03:53:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14015s) note that your inside view estimate will

[03:53:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14016s) almost always be way off. So this is

[03:53:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14018s) necessary for any stakeholders in a

[03:53:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14020s) project not to hit your guts. You see

[03:53:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14022s) this a lot when you pitch a client on

[03:53:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14024s) timets and timelines for a project. You

[03:53:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14027s) know I do a lot of a automation and so a

[03:53:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14029s) lot of the time I say something along

[03:53:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14030s) the lines of this will take 2 to 3

[03:53:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14032s) weeks. How long do I actually think

[03:53:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14033s) it'll take? I think it'll take about a

[03:53:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14035s) week. But I add on okay an additional

[03:53:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14037s) 1.5 to 2x amount of time just because I

[03:54:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14040s) know that my internal estimates are not

[03:54:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14043s) to be trusted.

[03:54:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14047s) a project in isolation and instead begin

[03:54:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14050s) similar to that project. Whereas before

[03:54:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14053s) we talked about the inside view which is

[03:54:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14060s) Instead of how long is my course going

[03:54:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14062s) to take to write it is how long did the

[03:54:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14064s) last three courses I took to write take.

[03:54:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14073s) doing is you're averaging out the actual

[03:57:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14224s) explore exploit trade-off. Now, imagine

[03:57:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14226s) you're in a casino and you're presented

[03:57:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14228s) with 10 awesome looking fancy slot

[03:57:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14230s) machines. Every machine has a different

[03:57:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14232s) payout rate, but you don't know which

[03:57:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14234s) machine is which. And what you have is

[03:57:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14236s) 100 poles to distribute amongst each of

[03:57:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14238s) the possible slot machines. Every pull

[03:57:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14241s) on a machine that you know pays out

[03:57:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14243s) well, aka an exploit of a machine that

[03:57:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14246s) you know is going to pay you, costs you

[03:57:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14248s) a pull that you technically could have

[03:57:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14250s) used to discover an even better machine

[03:57:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14252s) elsewhere. This technically is called

[03:57:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14254s) exploring.

[03:57:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14255s) [gasps] On the flip side, every pull on

[03:57:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14257s) a machine that you don't know the payout

[03:57:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14258s) of, aka ones that you are exploring,

[03:57:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14260s) will cost you pulls on a machine that

[03:57:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14262s) you do know pays out well. This is a

[03:57:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14265s) classic problem in research and

[03:57:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14266s) development and also in planning and

[03:57:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14268s) acting throughout your life. How many of

[03:57:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14270s) these machines should I pull before I

[03:57:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14273s) should feel satisfied with what I have

[03:57:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14275s) and just pull the highest performer?

[03:57:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14278s) This is called the exploit explorer

[03:58:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14280s) trade-off.

[03:58:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14282s) You know, should I do what works or

[03:58:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14284s) should I try something new?

[03:58:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14287s) Exploiting is always about milking a

[03:58:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14289s) known good. Exploring is about spending

[03:58:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14292s) time and accepting sub-optimal outcomes.

[03:58:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14294s) If you think about it from a local

[03:58:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14296s) maximum perspective, this is where you

[03:58:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14297s) descend your local maximum. To learn

[03:58:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14300s) more about other options that might be

[03:58:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14301s) better performing than the ones that

[03:58:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14303s) you're currently exploiting.

[03:58:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14304s) Now, super annoyingly, I find both of

[03:58:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14306s) these are seen as virtues in the real

[03:58:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14307s) world, which prevents a frank and honest

[03:58:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14309s) discussion about how to proceed. For

[03:58:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14311s) instance, somebody who never explores is

[03:58:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14313s) usually called focused or specialized.

[03:58:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14316s) And then when their thing, whatever it

[03:58:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14318s) is that they're doing, dries up because

[03:58:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14324s) because they've never innovated or grew

[03:58:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14326s) past that. On the other hand, somebody

[03:58:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14328s) who never exploits could be seen as

[03:58:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=14331s) growthminded or always learning for a

[04:34:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16493s) a premortem? Very simple trick. Just

[04:34:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16495s) change the tense. Instead of post, it's

[04:34:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16497s) pre. Instead of asking why did something

[04:35:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16500s) fail, you ask what did fail if I were to

[04:35:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16506s) have done this and it failed.

[04:35:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16508s) So for instance, let's say you have an

[04:35:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16510s) idea to start a project and the

[04:35:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16512s) project's going to take you a week.

[04:35:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16513s) Rather than asking, "So what could go

[04:35:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16516s) wrong here?" or rather than waiting

[04:35:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16517s) until the project fails and then asking,

[04:35:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16519s) "So what went wrong here?" you could

[04:35:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16520s) just say, "So let's pretend it's one

[04:35:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16522s) week from today and the project failed.

[04:35:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16524s) Why did it fail?"

[04:35:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16526s) This is an extremely clear empirically

[04:35:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16530s) proven way of increasing the quality of

[04:35:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16533s) predictions that people make in a

[04:35:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16534s) project and the probability that they

[04:35:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16536s) are correct with their predictions as

[04:35:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16537s) well. Why? There are many explanations

[04:35:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16540s) for why. The first is when you ask the

[04:35:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16542s) question what might fail it's a very

[04:35:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16544s) abstract question and your [snorts]

[04:35:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16546s) brain treats it like an abstract

[04:35:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16547s) question and produces a very abstract

[04:35:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16548s) answer. The reality is that human beings

[04:35:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16550s) do not dwell in the abstract world. We

[04:35:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16553s) don't dwell on hypotheticals or

[04:35:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16554s) conditional probabilities. That's why we

[04:35:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16556s) have to learn what Baze theorem is. We

[04:35:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16558s) have to learn about expected value and

[04:36:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16560s) we sort of have to add that onto our

[04:36:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16561s) biology. The place that human beings

[04:36:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16563s) dwell is in stories. It's in narratives

[04:36:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16566s) and it's in explanations. So when you

[04:36:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16568s) ask why did the project fail instead of

[04:36:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16575s) details. It'll create characters, events

[04:36:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16580s) failure cases like you know somebody

[04:36:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16581s) forgot to send an email etc. What you do

[04:36:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16584s) is you do this so you everybody asks how

[04:36:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16586s) how and why did the project fail? It did

[04:36:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16589s) fail a week from now so why why did it

[04:36:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16599s) failure case. Like if a bunch of stories

[04:36:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16600s) have to do with people not sending

[04:36:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16602s) emails when they should have now you

[04:36:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16608s) of all possible failures alongside clear

[04:36:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16610s) solutions that ultimately improve

[04:36:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16614s) called the premortem loop. You write the

[04:36:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16615s) plan. You pretend it's a week later and

[04:36:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16617s) it failed. Then you write the story in

[04:36:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16619s) the past tense. Everybody brings their

[04:37:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16622s) the causes. You do this over and over

[04:37:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16624s) and over and over again until your

[04:37:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QLLuZbuTIRc&t=16625s) project is airtight.
